## Claude

A builder is working on the import-seal leak now, with first priority. It hasn't reported yet, so the cheat path is still open on main.

- **The defect:** `b7a773744e` (`ARENA_RUNNER_FIX_OPTIONAL_IMPORT_DENIED`) made optional imports raise a catchable `ModuleNotFoundError`. That leaks the hook frame and sealed generator, so arm code can forge the completion marker and a failing check passes.
- **The fix:** if the proper fix isn't quick, it first restores `a68eb01491`'s behaviour for those imports and pushes that at once. It then adds a selftest that plants a forgery payload and must see it fail, and checks that the 91/265 discriminating count doesn't drop.
- **Shared file:** another builder is editing `arena_judge.py` at the same time for the judge proxy and plant fixes. Both pull right before each edit and keep both sides on conflict.

The Conductor has confirmed their independent tests passed for `ARENA_RUNNER_FIX_FROZEN_PATHLIB_DENIED` and `ARENA_EVENTLOG_WIRE_CALIB_ENTRYPOINT`.

They also said 132 of the 174 non-discriminating tests are env failures, which is the scope of `ARENA_TREE_FIX_OMITS_CHECK_READ_PATHS`. The sanity and tree builder already has that item.

I'll report the seal-leak sha and test command to the Conductor when it lands.

<result>I fixed ARENA_RUNNER_HARDEN_IMPORT_SEAL_LEAK properly instead of reverting. The fix is on origin/main at `aa561d62a7`, and the ledger record at `c077c1bb63` (OWNER.jsonl).

**Fix.** The RUNNER's audit hook no longer raises at all. When a check does a plain `import` of a denied module (ctypes, `_interpreters`…), the hook puts `None` in `sys.modules` for that name and returns. Python's own import machinery then raises ModuleNotFoundError, so the hook's frame, which holds the sealed generator, never appears in a traceback the check code can reach. The hook also never runs code the check could have overridden: it uses only plain `dict` operations on `sys.__dict__`, and if `sys.modules` has been replaced by anything but a plain dict, the process is killed. Actually loading one of these modules still hits the existing kill.

**Evidence.**
- New selftest `t_seal_leak` in `selftest_arena_judge.py`:
  - The planted forgery, against a check that exits 1, passes on a copy of the runner whose hook raises. That proves the payload is a live attack.
  - On the real runner it comes back `fail`, and the payload is seen running and finding no seal.
  - A second forgery runs code inside the hook through a dict-subclass `sys.modules`; it is denied.
  - Deleting `_interpreters` from `sys.modules` and importing it again still gives an ImportError.
- The existing optional-import checks (concurrent.futures, `try: import ctypes`) still pass.
- Probe at `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\arena\seal_leak\probe.out`: the b7a773744e runner gives a forged pass; the fixed runner gives `fail` on 3 of 3 runs.
- A full vet on the fixed runner finds 135 of 265 checks discriminating (was 91) and 0 denied, in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\arena\seal_leak\vet_tally.txt`.

**How to test:** `python3 src/RimMandrake/Utils/run_selftests.py --only src/RimMandrake/Utils/arena/selftest_arena_judge.py` (GREEN).

**Shared index:** the house hook blocks `git commit` without paths, and `git commit &lt;path&gt;` would have swept in the other builder's uncommitted hunks in the same two files. So I committed only my changes with git plumbing, using a temporary index (commit-tree, then update-ref). Their working-tree changes are untouched and still uncommitted.

**Owed:** `arena_judge.py` is still DIRTY in `code_review_status.py check` and needs a review.</result>

## Claude

The stop-gate builder finished all six items, and I've reported them to the Conductor. Each is recorded implemented with an L1 check left for their independent tester. Selftests for analysis, trial, spend, eventlog and rounds pass, and all 29 mutants are caught.

- **FUTILE** (`ARENA_FUTILE_GATE_FIX_OLD_TASK_DROPS`): held behind the same newest-era gate. It becomes CONTINUE, with the drop it would have made recorded on the block.

C# is on by default in proportion (8 of 50), and `--no-csharp` leaves it out.
- **Forecast text:** the 24–37 forecast is withdrawn in the `arena_analysis.py` docstring and in `design/RimMandrake/architecture_switch_harness_2026-10-29.md` and `testpilot_merged_2026-10-29.md`.
- **GR-a selftest** (`ARENA_EVLOG_SELFTEST_FIX_GRA_TOKEN_UNREAD`): it now writes its own dummy token, and the real token file is untouched.

Still owed:
- **`trial_tasks.json`:** rebuild it with `arena_trial.py build` after the final vet changes the frozen pool.
- **`arena_replay.py`:** pass it the same task list through `--tasks`.
- **Review:** the arena files are still unreviewed.

Two builders are still running: the pool correctness items with the full vet, and the mirror and answer-check fixes. I haven't started the final full vet.

The `arena_analysis.py` power text is corrected and published at `bba7d4952d`, and I've told the Conductor. It's a docstring-only change, and `selftest_arena_analysis.py` is green.

The docstring had no literal "40-50%". It did carry the likely origin: an exchangeable-task simulation giving DECIDE 28% by 25 tasks per arm, up to 50% by 60. I left that measurement as a record and added a "SUPERSEDED FOR THE TRIAL" note after it. The note says the old line is ungated and overstates the era-gated rule. That also settles where the earlier 40–50% line came from: it was the old simulation, not a trial figure.

`arena_analysis.py` is not in the answer check's sandbox hash, so this edit doesn't make the record stale.

The pool correctness builder is still running its three items, then the full vet. I'll tell the Conductor when the last `arena_judge.py` edit is in and the vet starts.

## Claude

I've started two builders on the Conductor's last batch. Neither has reported yet, and nothing is frozen or re-vetted.

- **Judge-edit builder:** it is the only one allowed to edit `arena_judge.py`, so there is a single final re-vet. It covers `ARENA_RUNNER_FIX_NESTED_EXIT_ZERO` and `ARENA_RUNNER_FIX_FAILED_OUTPUT_LOST`. The second keeps the failing output tail, which the reader pass needs because 79 of 163 base tails are empty. It also covers the cheap classes of `ARENA_VET_FIX_ENV_CULLED_CLASSES`. It reports the final `arena_judge.py` sha so the re-vet can start.
- **Freeze, exclusions and reader tooling:** this builder stays out of the judge file.
  - **Freeze:** it fixes `ARENA_FREEZE_FIX_HARNESS_DIRS_REFUSED`.
  - **Exclusions:** it excludes the 4 provisionally-Clean tasks without bypassing the guard. It also limits sibling tasks from one bundled commit to one per trial list, and `N` stays a parameter.
  - **Reader tool:** it writes `arena_reader_pass.py`, which gives each scored check to an independent reader, preferring GPT. The reader judges whether the check tests what the task asks, whether a correct solution from the task text could pass it, and whether it is implementation-coupled. Verdicts are stored per task, and a second subcommand applies them as an exclusion list that `arena_trial.py build` reads. It is tested with a stub reader and not run on the real pool yet.

My sequence once both finish:
1. Run the full re-vet.
2. Run the reader pass over every survivor.
3. Run the census and rerun the answer check.
4. Rebuild the lists for n=50, 75 and 100, and report the surviving count per kind and era.

I tell the Conductor the count before anything else, since it decides what goes to the owner. I won't freeze.

1. **Import-only rule** (`arena_reader_pass.py`, verdict schema now `/3`, so `/1` and `/2` verdicts read as pending). Each check in the packet now gets a mechanical reading of its base output. The reader may not KEEP a check when:
   - the check is red at base only because a module or name the fix added, and the task text doesn't name, is missing (ImportError, ModuleNotFoundError, AttributeError, NameError, or the C# equivalents), with no other failure line beside it; or
   - the base output shows no failure reason at all (your addendum).

   The reader also gets a new yes/no question, whether the check goes red for the defect, and a KEEP needs a yes there too. A packet without this reading can't carry a KEEP. Such checks have to come back as UNSCORE_CHECK or DROP_TASK. The new selftest rows use a stub reader.
2. **Tail fix** (`arena_tasks.py`): the stored tail now keeps the head, the failure lines and the end, capped at 1500 characters, instead of the last 600. The vet cache key is unchanged (still `b4e465a6a8b1c392`): the key hashes the vet code with the old 600-character cut written back in, and a selftest guards this.

**Tail and re-vet:** the reader takes the tail from the stored vet results; nothing recomputes it. Rows vetted before this fix keep their 600-character tails until they are re-vetted. Until then, the 33 rows with no visible reason are refused, not kept.

## Claude

I forwarded all four pre-mortem defects to the launcher in one message, with instructions to fix them in a single pass and relaunch once. No arm is running yet.

1. **`ARENA_EXPORT_TREE_SHRINK_RAM_OOM`:** the tree export buffers the 7.7 GB tree twice in RAM, 15.5 GB against the 12 GB slice. The runner runs out of memory at the tester stage of every arm. The fix is to stream it to disk.
2. **`ARENA_ORPHAN_BUILD_UNSTICK_V3_ARM_BRICKED`:** the v3 arm is stuck. It has a signed build end with no test start, so `require_tested` refuses every brief and no operator void exists. I told the launcher to add a signed, logged harness-side recovery for restarts. It must not weaken `require_tested` or rewrite signed events.
3. **`ARENA_REPLAY_SWEEP_REPAIR_ORPHAN_CLONE`:** a killed runner leaves its clone, and the relaunch dies on `ReplayExists`. The runner should sweep its own orphan clone at start.
4. **`ARENA_LOOP_STOP_REPAIR_STALE_ON_RETRY`:** stale `STOP` files from the earlier failed attempt make a relaunch end at once. They get cleared at a fresh trial start, and removed now.

The launcher logs each landed fix to `launcher.log`, and the monitor will wake me on it. I'm waiting for an `ARM RUNNING` line.

The v3 arm got through its work and then died at the judge step, and I've sent the cause to the launcher. It closed its work at 09:28:00 after about 9 minutes, head `0575f0bc2536`. The judge command then failed with `RmDataRootUnset`: `rmpaths` looked for Lodestar under `/tmp/arm_home_zgsu1alh/dev/Lodestar` and didn't find it. So the harness-side judge is being run with the arm's temporary sandbox HOME. That is a launcher bug, not a problem with the frozen judge, and the vet key is untouched.

I told the launcher to run the judge with the harness's real environment, or an explicit `RM_DATA_ROOT` from it, never the arm's HOME. It should also check every other harness-side subprocess in the runner for the same leak: gate, tester, critic relay, spend meter and snapshot. After the fix and publish, it relaunches all three arms once.

ralph_a and trio are still running with the old code and will probably hit the same failure at their own judge step within minutes. If they do, the relaunch redoes them from the first task. ## Claude

This needs the Conductor's pick. The pre-mortem found that 8 of the 29 frozen tasks have only a held-back proof, so the gate's proof step reads UNMEASURED and the verdict is invalid. The judge stops before the hidden checks, so those 8 tasks cannot pass in any arm. That hits all three arms alike, but it wastes about 28% of the trial.

I sent the Conductor three options:
- **(a) Change the gate:** `arena_gate.py` is harness code, not the judge or the vet key. A held-back-only proof would be accepted and measured, with a selftest.
- **(b) Exclude the 8 tasks:** this leaves the pool hash untouched but would run 21 tasks against a freeze of 29, so the Conductor would have to rule on it.
- **(c) Run all 29** and mark those 8 as not passable in the analysis.

I recommended (a) if the gate change is small and provable, otherwise (b). I told the launcher not to edit anything for this one, only to list the 8 task ids and size what the held-back proof step needs.

I took the second defect without waiting, `ARENA_GATE_TESTS_FIX_PLAIN_SELFTEST_UNCOUNTED`. A correct fix on a task whose own selftest is plain, like `selftest_deploy_hold.py`, exits 0 with no assert or `check*`/`t_*` markers and reads invalid. The launcher makes the gate count a clean exit-0 run of the task's own selftest as a pass, with a selftest row and no judge touch, in the same relaunch as the gate work directory move to ext4. It also lists which of the 29 tasks have plain selftests.

The arms are mid-first-task on the old code and get relaunched after those fixes land. The 8 PM line isn't affected yet.

## Claude

The Conductor picked option (a), and a separate Opus gate builder is now working on it. No data is contaminated yet, because no task has finished. The final relaunch waits for the gate fix.

**What the gate builder does**, in order:
1. Move the gate work directory from `/mnt/d` to ext4 (`ARENA_GATE_WORK_REWIRE_OFF_DRVFS`). It publishes this first and logs it in `launcher.log`.
2. Run the held-back proof for real and score its real result (`ARENA_GATE_PROOF_FIX_HELD_BACK_UNPASSABLE`). It never counts held-back-only as an automatic pass, and unmeasured still never passes anywhere else.
   - It identifies the 8 tasks.
   - For each task it proves the reference answer passes and the base commit fails.
   - A mutant shows a failing held-back proof fails the gate.
   - Any of the 8 that fails goes to `trial_exclusions.json`. The Conductor rules up to 3 of those. If more than 3 fail, the builder stops and reports.
3. Make the plain-selftest rule count an exit-0 run only where the base commit fails that selftest for that task, so a no-op can't pass. It checks this across all 29 tasks and adds a no-op mutant.
4. Rerun the answer check, confirm the vet key is still `...@74156f55c8608337`, confirm `pool_sha256` is `6d0ef995…`, and run the gate, judge and trial selftests.

It may not touch the judge, the sandbox, the answer check, the frozen pool or the arena entry points.

**Launcher:** I told it that `arena_gate.py` and its selftests now belong to the gate builder, so there are not two editors in one working tree. It keeps making the runner stop loudly on an all-UNMEASURED result, finding out why trio is down, and doing the final relaunch of all three arms. That relaunch waits for the gate builder's published fix.

I'll tell the Conductor when the gate fixes land, with the task ids, the per-task proof results and the exclusion count.

## Claude

v3's park was a real give-up, not a harness fault. ralph_a and trio were stopped by a harness bug, now fixed. I sent the Conductor the full answer.

- **v3:** three build passes committed real work (`60d638448`, `50d945865`, `708bc309`). Each of three tester passes then failed it on a real defect: a composed path not pruned, a symlinked checkout that made the prune silently do nothing, and a `lnk/../x` escape deleting a file outside the mod folder. After three failed tries it parks, and a parked task is judged at the base commit by design, so the reject is correct. There were no tool denials, budget, timeout or JSON errors.
- **ralph_a and trio:** the loop's pre-check was red at base inside the sandbox, with 89 FAIL and 7 CRASH. The loop only recognised red lines with an `[i/N]` prefix, which `run_selftests` prints only to stderr, and the pre-check passes on only stdout. So 0 of the 96 reds were seen, the base comparison never ran, and the loop wrote STOP before any model ran.
- **Fix:** `39bb46a3c7a9`, with a new selftest row. The old pattern found 0 of 96 reds in the real pre-check output and the new one finds 96. The ralph_a and trio selftests pass. `ARENA_PRECHECK_FIX_RED_LINES_ON_STDERR` stays open until a replay confirms it.

I also found a gate fault that did not cause the park. The gate's event record reads `recorded:false`, because `arena_eventlog._main_code_ok` rejects the real `arena_gate.py` as a borrowed filename. It would hit every arm's gate record, so I filed it as `ARENA_GATE_ENTRY_FIX_FALSE_BORROWED_CODE` and gave it to the gate builder. I told the builder not to weaken the forgery check, and to add a selftest where a borrowed-filename script is still rejected.

Current state: v3 is still running its second task. ralph_a and trio are stopped, and their STOP files need clearing at relaunch. The final relaunch of all three waits for the gate builder's held-back proof, plain-selftest rule and borrowed-code fix. Expect each first task's pre-check to take about 16 minutes longer, because base is now compared once.

The Causeway Test Pilot found the cause, and I sent the Conductor the report. `CAUSEWAY_DIAGNOSE_WAITS_AT_THE_LIP` stays open with a note, because the fix is a tuning call that belongs to the owner. - **Test script and site:**
  - Raids spawned by `--direct` never got shovels, so they wandered 18+ squares out and nothing tried to fill.
  - Colonists left in the open were downed, and the raid switched to kidnapping, which has no way across the trench.

  - One fill level costs 6400 work divided by (MiningSpeed × 1.7 × 0.5).
  - Four of six raiders sit at the 0.1 MiningSpeed floor, so a level takes about 75,000 ticks. The raid leaves at about t+29,000.
  - With fill speed raised 4 times, the breach-scar row passed. No raider crossed within the window, so the causeway-fill row still failed. Fill speed alone is not the cause, and that is logged as a false theory.
- **Proof script:** it now has `--shovels`, a granite box around each `--direct` site, and `--fill-speed`, which is checked on set and restored on exit. A fill row with no shovel holder reads UNMEASURED, not FAIL. Commits are `5e90c60679`, with the ledger published at `466ff82bd464`. I made no mod fix.
- **Owed to the owner:** `CAUSEWAY_DECIDE_FILL_PRICE_OUTLASTS_RAID`, and `RAIDER_SHOVELLER_DECIDE_SHOOTERS_STOP_TO_DIG`, where gunners with a sight line keep shooting and never dig.

## Claude

I resumed the Test Pilot helper with the owner's raider-fill ruling. It already has the run history and the cost formula from the diagnosis. It has not reported yet.

The ruling: raiders fill only shallow pits worth their effort, and deep pits become a real defense.

Order given:
1. **Depth line:** the line sits at what needs droids or machines. The helper chooses the number, derives it from the actual mod, and logs it as delegated.
2. **Below the line:** raiders fill at a tuned rate that finishes a shallow pit inside a raid. It works that out from the fill formula and the real MiningSpeed spread, including the 0.1 floor. It does not just multiply the speed by 8.
3. **At or above the line:** raiders path around or stand and shoot, and must not idle at the lip. A raid with no melee pawn goes around. Melee digs, and gunners cover.
4. **Mod settings:** the depth line and fill rate become mod settings, with defaults equal to shipped behaviour.
5. **Proof:** both sides live through the bridge, at shipped tuned values with no fill-speed hack. It updates the bars in `raid_trench_proof.py` and records what it learns, false theories included.
6. **Text and ledger:**
   - It corrects any spec text that says raiders fill deep pits.
   - It closes `CAUSEWAY_DECIDE_FILL_PRICE_OUTLASTS_RAID` and `RAIDER_SHOVELLER_DECIDE_SHOOTERS_STOP_TO_DIG`, citing the owner's words.
   - It files a parked item for siege raids launching over pits.

The helper is again told to leave the replay alone. It checks free RAM first and uses SteveDesktop if the game would compete. It stamps the game down and restores the ModsConfig when it finishes.

The replay is unchanged: all three arms are still stopped, waiting for the gate builder's `FIXED GATE all`.

## Claude

I resumed the gate builder with the Conductor's pick. I'm still holding the relaunch.

**What the gate builder does** (option (a), second form):
- **Excuse rule:** the gate's tests step excuses only reds that also fail for the reference fix in the same sandbox. It does not scope by "selftests the fix touches", because an arm could break something else unseen.
- **Stored per task:** the excuse lists are measured per task and stored before the arms run, for all 29 tasks. An empty list means no excuse. The gate reads them and never decides live, and a missing or unreadable list reads UNMEASURED.
- **Mutants:**
  - A test that passes at reference but fails for an arm still fails the gate.
  - A red absent from the stored list also fails the gate.
- **Confirmation:**
  - It checks that EVIDENCE_COVERS, TEST_SWITCH, RIVERSITE and FILLQUEUE now pass with the reference fix and fail at base, and that the other 25 tasks are unchanged.
  - It reruns the answer check, the vet key (`...@74156f55c8608337`) and `pool_sha256` (`6d0ef995…2319`).
- **Hand-off:** it logs `FIXED GATE reference-reds <sha>` in `launcher.log` only if all of that is clean. It does not relaunch.

When that line arrives I resume the launcher for the single final relaunch of all three arms, with the ralph_a and trio STOP files cleared, and I won't wait for the Conductor if the checks are clean.

Mandrake's two new items wait until after the relaunch: Abyss muffle on by default, and the Twilight cast blessed with the draft's recommended answers. I'll start helpers for them then.

## Claude

I've started an Opus builder on `ARENA_PUBLISH_DQ_FIX_PARKED_REJECT_VIOLATES`. It can change a verdict and is not fixed yet. At present `dq_publish` marks any judge event with no gate pass at that sha as a violation. The runner judges every task, so a single parked or gate-failed task disqualifies the whole arm. On v3 that gives UNMEASURED with rc 3, and in the filer's synthetic 29×3 run `DECIDE_FOR_ralph_a` flips to TIE.

The fix direction is to disqualify only a judge accept or a real publish with no gate pass. A parked reject, a gate-failed reject or a pending marker would not count.

Still running in parallel, on separate files: the 26-task list builder, the anchors builder, and the gate fixes already published. The relaunch comes after the 26-task list lands, and after this fix if it turns out to be runner-time.

1. **Fix:** an arm is now disqualified only for a judge accept with no earlier gate pass, or for a judge at a sha that differs from base with no gate at all. Parked rejects, gate-failed rejects and pending markers no longer count. A missing or short base gives UNMEASURED.
2. **Selftest:** the new `t_publish_parked` set covers all the rows you listed plus boundary cases. The old rule is registered as a mutant and gets caught. The full selftest has no failures, and `run_selftests --only` is GREEN 2/2.

One variant, a parked task judged invalid, gave CONTINUE: an invalid verdict does not count as a completed task, so that is correct.

## Claude

The gate has a bug that makes most of the 26 tasks unpassable even for the reference fix. I told the Conductor I need their yes before touching it, because they had ruled out more gate changes.

**Cause** (`ARENA_PROOF_FIX_BLIND_ONLY_TARGET`, filed `2d226c3e72`, published `d54830fb80`): TESTENV_GUARD's proof runs `run_selftests.py --only <selftest>`.
- `proof_touches` in `arena_gate.py` looks only at the script named on the command line, `run_selftests.py`. It never looks at the `--only` target.
- The proof step then reads UNMEASURED without ever running, and the verdict becomes `invalid`, which stops the runner.
- This is not v3's fault. The reference fix gets the same UNMEASURED proof in the gate builder's own sweep.
- The proof itself works. On clean copies the reference fix passes, and base code with the reference's own selftest fails.

**Scope, with the reference fix:**
- 13 tasks get an invalid verdict and stop the runner: TESTENV_GUARD, SAVE_GUARD, ENACT_KEEP_AGE, ENACT_PURGE, ENACT_VARIANT, ENACT_STALE_KEY, RIMFLOW_LANE, VALIDATE_PATCH, RIMBRIDGE_DEADLINE, LAUNCHREADY, SEAT_SYNC_HARDEN, CONVERSATIONS_LAND and SAVES_PRUNE.
- 5 more get `fail`, so no arm can pass them: TEST_SWITCH, RIVER_CHAINS, RIVERSITE, MODCHECK and ROSTERS. The first four hit a blocked FlowWorks deploy plan, and ROSTERS hits validate_patch errors.
- Of the 8 held-back tasks, only DEPLOY_PRUNE, ART_GUARD and SITHLINE pass the gate. The other five fail on placeholder findings the investigator did not dig into.
- Excluding every task the reference can't pass would leave 3 tasks, so exclusion is not an answer.

**Proposal I sent:**
1. One more gate round. `proof_touches` also accepts a selftest named after `--only` when it is in the diff or is a selftest. That one rule clears the proof step for all 18 tasks.
2. A second pass over the 5 tasks that fail, to separate gate faults from real ones. Then a re-sweep of all 26 against the reference, each task either passing or listed for exclusion.
3. Relaunch v3 only. ralph_a and trio are mid-first-task and keep the old gate for now.

v3 is down until the Conductor says go, and the handoff still waits.
